\subsection{The Quadratic Relation and the Factor of Two}

On the isolated-system branch, in static isotropic coordinates, the spacetime metric and local standards take the form
\begin{equation}
ds^2=-N^2c_0^2dt^2+A_{\rm iso}^2d\boldsymbol{\sigma}^2,\qquad
p_T=N,\quad p_L=A_{\rm iso}^{-1},\quad
d\tau=p_Tdt,\quad d\ell=\frac{d\sigma}{p_L}.
\end{equation}
Here $A_{\rm iso}$ denotes the spatial metric factor (distinguishing it from the cosmological scale factor $A$ of Section~3.3). The null interval condition for light ($ds^2 = 0$) yields
\begin{equation}
\frac{c_{\rm coord}}{c_0}=p_Tp_L,\qquad
c_{\rm local}=\frac{d\ell}{d\tau}=c_0.
\end{equation}
The common metric unites material clocks, rulers, and light within a single local geometry. In general, the factors $p_T$ and $p_L$ are determined by independent field equations. In an asymptotically flat, weak spherically symmetric exterior region, to first order in the dimensionless potential $\epsilon=GM/(c_0^2r)$, the two factors coincide: $p_T=1-\epsilon+O(\epsilon^2)$ and $p_L=1-\epsilon+O(\epsilon^2)$. This coincidence characterizes the leading weak-field approximation, whereas at higher orders and in the matter interior $p_T$ and $p_L$ remain independent variables.

In the common-scaling ansatz, the exact equality $p_T=p_L=p$ holds. Under this condition, the external coordinate footprint of a local ruler and the coordinate clock rate both scale by the factor $p$, while the clock period scales as $p^{-1}$; on this specialized branch, the coordinate speed of light therefore equals $p^2 c_0$.

Accordingly, on the common-scaling branch, the biconformal scaling law takes the form
\begin{equation}
\frac{L_{\rm coord}}{L_0} = \frac{m_{\rm eff}}{m_0} = \frac{\Omega_t}{\Omega_{t,0}} = p, \qquad \frac{c_{\rm coord}}{c_0} = p^2, \qquad p = e^{\varphi/2}.
\end{equation}

Here $L_{\rm coord}$ denotes the footprint of a local ruler on the distant asymptotic coordinate grid, while in its own environment the observer continues to read it as one unchanged unit. The clock law gives $d\tau=p\,dt$, and ruler calibration gives $d\ell=p^{-1}d\sigma$. Consequently, the temporal metric coefficient is $p^2$, the spatial coefficient is $p^{-2}$, the coordinate speed of light is $p^2c_0$, and the speed of light measured with the local clock and ruler remains exactly $c_0$.

A similar power-law dependence appears historically in the polarizable-vacuum (PV) models of Robert Dicke and Harold Puthoff \citep{dicke1957,puthoff2002}. $\RefG$ connects these exponents to the ontological distinction between foundation nodes and their connecting links.

Formally, the externally determined effective mass of a single oscillon depends on the foundation's pressure-deficit profile through the exponential law $e^{\varphi/2}$:
\begin{equation}
m_{\rm eff} = m_0 e^{\varphi/2}.
\end{equation}

On the deficit branch, $\varphi < 0$ and $0 < p < 1$. The relation $m_{\rm eff} = p m_0$ expresses the gravitationally redshifted value of a fixed proper energy in mass units. In the static metric of the full coframe, the test-body Lagrangian expands as $L = -mc_0^2 N + \tfrac12 m A_{\rm iso}^2 N^{-1}v^2 + O(mv^4/c_0^2)$, showing that the external rest energy and the coordinate inertial coefficient are distinct quantities. The physical inertia of an entire isolated body is determined in Section~2.3 by the total energy--momentum of the system.

Four physical quantities are distinguished in the energy accounting:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Oscillon internal state and proper energy} ($M_{\rm proper}$);
\item \textbf{Single-oscillon effective refractive mass in a given background field} ($m_{\rm eff}$);
\item \textbf{Source profile of the pressure deficit established in the foundation-medium} ($M_{\rm active}$);
\item \textbf{Total asymptotic gravitational charge of the entire system} (ADM and Komar total mass: \citealt{arnowitt1962,komar1959}).
\end{enumerate}

These four quantities define two levels of accounting: at the first level, the oscillon's proper internal energy is distinguished from the externally read contribution in a given gravitational environment; at the second level, the externally measured total mass combines the contributions of the active core and the medium. At the asymptotic boundary, they form a single total gravitational charge in which each contribution is counted once.

An oscillon is a localized resonant structure of the unified foundation, yet its resonant influence extends beyond the core into the surrounding foundation. The resonant fields of many oscillons overlap, encompass the entire foundation-medium, and lower its background pressure; $\RefG$ associates this collective process with background spatial relaxation.

In the common-$p$ relaxation scheme, growth of the region affected by the resonant interaction modifies local pressure, external energy, size readout, clock rate, and coordinate light speed. The negative feedback and homogeneous asymptotic relaxation of this scheme are analyzed within the corresponding scalar action and equations of state, whereas in the isolated covariant sector backreaction is governed by the joint field equations of the action $S_{\rm iso}$.

The common-scaling $p/p^2$ branch is described by the following biconformal metric (whose exponential form is a distinct special case compared alongside the general solution of the full coframe):
\begin{equation}
ds^2 = e^\varphi c^2 dt^2 - e^{-\varphi} d\boldsymbol{\sigma}^2, \qquad \varphi = -\frac{2U}{c^2},
\end{equation}
where $U>0$ is the gravitational potential of the selected exponential branch and coincides at leading order with the magnitude of the Newtonian potential. In the weak-field limit ($U/c^2 \ll 1$), the metric components expand in a Taylor series:
\begin{equation}
g_{00} = e^\varphi \approx 1 - \frac{2U}{c^2} + \frac{2U^2}{c^4} + \cdots, \qquad g_{ij} = -e^{-\varphi}\delta_{ij} \approx -\left(1 + \frac{2U}{c^2} + \cdots\right)\delta_{ij}.
\end{equation}

The shared clock--ruler scaling relations determine the biconformal metric and its leading spatial correction. The static profile of a massive source follows from a variational functional under three conditions: the response is additive and logarithmic ($u=-\ln p$), the gradient term is canonical, and the coupling to the active source is linear. Under these conditions, variation of the functional together with the asymptotic boundary condition yields the Newtonian profile $u=GM/(c^2r)$.

In this static, spherically symmetric weak-field description, the first post-Newtonian (1PN) metric expansion yields the Parameterized Post-Newtonian (PPN) coefficients:
\begin{equation}
\beta = 1, \qquad \gamma = 1.
\end{equation}

Here $\gamma=1$ is the geometric consequence of the equal first-order clock and ruler responses, while the second-order expansion of the exponential static response yields $\beta=1$.

Thus, self-regulation of the foundation-medium unites the state of matter, the pressure deficit, and the response of local clocks and rulers. Their complete dynamical description rests on a common geometry in which each energetic contribution from matter and the medium is counted exactly once.

\paragraph{Relational Coframe, Einstein--Hilbert Geometry, and Full 1PN.}

The covariant equations in this subsection are written in the $(-+++)$ metric signature (whereas the biconformal presentation above used the $(+---)$ signature).

The static $p$ profile represents only the volumetric scalar projection of the full geometry. The complete local geometric state is described by a \textbf{relational coframe} $e^A{}_\mu$---a set of local frame differential one-forms uniting volume, shape, shear, orientation, and temporal direction. The coframe uniquely defines the operational metric:
\begin{equation}
g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{AB}e^A{}_\mu e^B{}_\nu.
\end{equation}
The spacetime orientation of the coframe and the oscillon's internal three-axis structure are distinct physical structures (in the particle map, the color quantum number is associated with this internal three-axis structure). Neighboring coframes are compared through a flat, metric-compatible inertial connection, while the electromagnetic channel possesses its own $U(1)$ gauge connection. The non-closure of an infinitesimal contour under coframe transport is \textbf{geometric torsion}---the measure of non-integrable deformation in the coframe continuum.

In the post-Genesis relational-coframe continuum, three physical conditions determine the leading low-energy action:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item The action is local, time-reversal invariant (reversible), constant-coefficient, parity-even, and quadratic in the torsion tensor;
\item The phase sector is minimally coupled to the geometry, with no unsuppressed mixed operators participating at the same order;
\item The linear limit about a Minkowski background is regular, while the Lorentz orientation of the coframe remains an inertial gauge freedom---no independent long-range physical orientation wave is excited.
\end{enumerate}
Under these conditions, the unique admissible ratio of quadratic-invariant coefficients yields the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity (TEGR) combination. The three independent quadratic torsion invariants are
\begin{equation}
I_1=T^\rho{}_{\mu\nu}T_\rho{}^{\mu\nu},\qquad
I_2=T^\rho{}_{\mu\nu}T^{\nu\mu}{}_\rho,\qquad
I_3=T_\mu T^\mu,\quad T_\mu=T^\nu{}_{\nu\mu}.
\end{equation}
Their teleparallel (TEGR) combination is
\begin{equation}
T_{\rm TEGR}=\frac14 I_1+\frac12 I_2-I_3.
\end{equation}
Accordingly, on the cosmological phase branch, the action takes the form
\begin{equation}
S_F=-\frac{c_0^3}{16\pi G}\int d^4x\,e
\left(T_{\rm TEGR}+2\Lambda_F\right)
+S_C[g,J,\theta_C]+S_{\partial},
\end{equation}
where $S_C$ is the covariant phase-current action and $S_{\partial}$ is the boundary-matched functional. The minus sign preceding the gravitational torsion term combines with the torsion--curvature identity below to recover the canonical sign of the Einstein--Hilbert action.
In the $(-+++)$ metric signature, the exact differential-geometric identity
\begin{equation}
R_{\rm LC}=-T_{\rm TEGR}+\frac{2}{e}\partial_\mu(eT^\mu),
\end{equation}
converts the coframe action into the Einstein--Hilbert action up to a total divergence (boundary term) \citep{jimenez2020ngr,maluf2013tegr}. Here $R_{\rm LC}$ is the Ricci scalar curvature of the Levi--Civita connection, $e=\det(e^A{}_\mu)$ is the coframe determinant, and $T^\mu$ is the trace of the torsion tensor. The equivalence of the actions is exact for compactly supported variations under suitable asymptotic falloff conditions (or when the matched boundary term $S_\partial$ is included). Thus, Einstein geometry is the direct manifestation of the low-energy continuum dynamics of the relational coframe. The independent route of Fierz--Pauli linear spin-2 theory and Deser's universal self-coupling arrives at the same Einstein--Hilbert operator.

The action $S_C$ describes a barotropic, isentropic, and irrotational single-potential phase current on its positive, stable, and causal constitutive branch:
\begin{equation}
\rho_C>0,\qquad \rho_C'>0,\qquad
0\le n_{C,{\rm op}}\rho_C''(n_{C,{\rm op}})/\rho_C'(n_{C,{\rm op}})\le1.
\end{equation}
Variation with respect to the phase $\theta_C$ yields the conserved current, while variation with respect to the metric yields the Hilbert stress--energy tensor \citep{brown1993fluid}:
\begin{equation}
T^C_{\mu\nu}=(\rho_C+p_C)u_\mu u_\nu+p_Cg_{\mu\nu}.
\end{equation}
Here $n_{C,{\rm op}}$ is the phase-charge density per unit operational volume, and $p_C=n_{C,{\rm op}}\rho_C'(n_{C,{\rm op}})-\rho_C(n_{C,{\rm op}})$ is the thermodynamic pressure of the phase sector. The quantity $P_F$, by contrast, represents the operational readout of the foundation's background pressure; these are two distinct physical variables whose connecting equation of state is determined by the microphysics of the foundation. When the source is restricted to the phase current alone, the field equation takes the form
\begin{equation}
G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda_Fg_{\mu\nu}
=\frac{8\pi G}{c_0^4}T^C_{\mu\nu}.
\end{equation}
In the general case, the geometry is sourced by the total stress--energy tensor $T^{\rm tot}_{\mu\nu}$, in which each physical sector of the action enters exactly once through its own metric variation.

The low-energy sector of an isolated system ($S_{\rm iso}$) in units with $c_0=\hbar=1$ takes the form
\begin{equation}
S_{\rm iso}=\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\left[
\frac{R}{16\pi G}
-g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\Psi_O^*\partial_\nu\Psi_O
-U(|\Psi_O|^2)-\frac14F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}
\right]+S_{\partial},\qquad \Lambda=0.
\end{equation}
Here the canonical normalization is $\Psi_O=f e^{i\theta_O}/\sqrt2$ and $U(|\Psi_O|^2)=V(f)$. In the dimensionless units employed, the self-interaction potential is $V(f)=f^2/2-f^4/4+f^6/24$, and in amplitude--phase variables the matter Lagrangian takes the form $-\tfrac12(\partial f)^2-\tfrac12f^2(\partial\theta_O)^2-V(f)$. The tensor $F_{\mu\nu}$ is the Maxwell electromagnetic field strength. For a neutral oscillon in equilibrium, the background electromagnetic field vanishes, while small electromagnetic excitations propagate on the same metric light cone. The metric, matter field, and Maxwell field are varied independently, and a specific coordinate system is chosen after the equations of motion are obtained (introducing an independent scalar $u$-field corresponds to a different action).

In the initial hydrodynamic formulation $S_C[g, J_C, \theta_C]$, the cosmological phase sector was defined only in the domain of nonvanishing density ($n_{C,{\rm op}} > 0$): because the rest chemical potential is positive ($\mu_{C0} = \rho_C'(0) > 0$), the derivative $\partial\rho_C/\partial J_C^\mu = \mu_{C0}\,u_{C\,\mu}$ is direction-dependent (discontinuous) in the limit of vanishing current ($J_C^\mu \to 0$), and in an inhomogeneous static gravitational field ($\nabla_i N \neq 0$) a stationary phase $\theta_C = -\omega_C t$ cannot simultaneously satisfy the eikonal threshold condition $\chi_C \equiv -g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\theta_C\partial_\nu\theta_C = \mu_{C0}^2$ throughout space. This analytical obstruction is resolved by replacing the hydrodynamic variables with a regular collective complex scalar field $\Psi_C = \sqrt{x}\,e^{i\theta_C}$ ($x \equiv |\Psi_C|^2$) governed by the Lagrangian
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{L}_C(\Psi_C,\nabla\Psi_C,g) = -\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu\Psi_C^*\nabla_\nu\Psi_C - U_C(|\Psi_C|^2), \qquad U_C(x) = \mu_{C0}^2 x + \frac{1}{2}\lambda_C x^2.
\end{equation}
Through this formulation, the isolated, cosmological, strong-field, and galactic sectors are unified within a single regular Grand Unified Covariant Action:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Strict Vacuum Reduction ($\Psi_C \equiv 0$, $\Lambda_F = 0$):} Because $\mathcal{L}_C$ is a smooth quadratic functional at $\Psi_C = 0$, when the collective field vanishes the unified action reduces \textbf{identically and without singularity} on any curved metric to the isolated-system action $S_{\rm iso}$;
\item \textbf{Active Cosmological Branch ($x > 0$):} In the high-frequency (Thomas--Fermi, $|\nabla\sqrt{x}|^2 \ll \chi_C x$) regime, the Legendre transformation $\chi_C = U_C'(x) = \mu_{C0}^2 + \lambda_C x$ exactly recovers the barotropic stress--energy tensor $T^C_{\mu\nu} = (\rho_C + p_C)u_\mu u_\nu + p_C g_{\mu\nu}$, where $\rho_C = \mu_{C0}^2 x + \frac{3}{4}\lambda_C x^2$ and $p_C = \frac{1}{4}\lambda_C x^2$, and in the dilute limit ($\lambda_C x \ll \mu_{C0}^2$) yields a pressureless cold-dust regime ($w_C \to 0$, $c_{s,C}^2 \to 0$). Deriving the parameters ($\mu_{C0}, \lambda_C$) of the effective potential $U_C(|\Psi_C|^2)$ from the microscopic dynamics of discrete foundation nodes remains a task for subsequent investigation;
\item \textbf{Grand Unified Covariant Action and the Common-Scale Ricci Total-Divergence Theorem:} Identifying the temporal foliation (clock) scalar with the collective condensate phase via $\Phi(x) \equiv -\frac{c_0}{\mu_{C0}}\theta_C(x)$ renders the medium four-velocity $u_\mu = -\partial_\mu\Phi/\sqrt{X} = \partial_\mu\theta_C/\sqrt{\chi_C}$ and the orthogonal spatial projector $\gamma^{\mu\nu} = g^{\mu\nu} + u^\mu u^\nu$ identical across all sectors, while the spatiotemporal medium deformation invariants ($\widehat{Y} \equiv X e^{-2H}/c_0^2$, $\widehat{B}^{AB} \equiv g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\Sigma^A\partial_\nu\Sigma^B e^{2H}$) and the galactic gradient prefactor share the unified scale $M_*^4 = \frac{M_{\rm Pl}^2 a_0^2}{2 c_0^4} = \frac{a_0^2}{16\pi G}$. On any static common-scaling metric $ds^2 = -e^{-2H(\mathbf{x})}c_0^2 dt^2 + e^{2H(\mathbf{x})}\delta_{ij}dx^i dx^j$ ($\Phi = c_0 t, \Sigma^A = x^A$), the invariants satisfy $\widehat{Y} \equiv 1$ and $\widehat{B}^{AB} \equiv \delta^{AB}$ identically, and the Einstein--Hilbert scalar curvature density is an \textbf{exact total spatial divergence} to all orders in $H(\mathbf{x})$:
\begin{equation}
\sqrt{-g}\,R \equiv 2\,\nabla^2 H = 2\,\partial_i(\delta^{ij}\partial_j H), \qquad \frac{\delta}{\delta H}\!\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\,R \equiv 0.
\end{equation}
Consequently, on the static common-scaling branch the Ricci scalar makes zero bulk Euler--Lagrange contribution to the $H$ field equation, which is governed entirely by the invariant medium functional $\mathcal{F}(Z_H/a_0^2)$ ($Z_H = c_0^4\gamma^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu H\nabla_\nu H$). In the strong field of compact objects ($r=2m$, $M=1\,M_\odot$, where $x_{\rm acc} = |\nabla\Phi|/a_0 \approx 7.69\times 10^{22} \gg 1$), the high-gradient limit $\mathcal{F}(x^2) \to x^2$ reduces the equation to the quadratic Laplace equation ($\nabla^2 H = 0 \implies H(r) = m/r$) with a fractional deviation of only $1.69\times 10^{-46}$, ensuring exact compatibility between the horizonless exponential strong-field metric and the galactic MOND/RAR regime ($x_{\rm acc} \lesssim 1$) within a single action.
\end{enumerate}

The selected teleparallel (TEGR) coframe action places $\RefG$ and General Relativity in the same first post-Newtonian (1PN) equivalence class when five conditions are satisfied:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item Through 1PN order, the complete action consists of the Einstein--Hilbert operator and the matter action;
\item Matter couples universally and minimally to the same metric;
\item The gravitational source is recorded by one complete, covariantly conserved stress--energy tensor;
\item $\RefG$ and General Relativity use the same local source, PPN gauge, asymptotic background, and boundary conditions;
\item No additional unsuppressed field degree of freedom or operator participates in the 1PN approximation.
\end{enumerate}
Under these conditions, the complete standard set of PPN parameters is
\begin{equation}
\gamma=\beta=1,\qquad
\xi=\alpha_1=\alpha_2=\alpha_3=
\zeta_1=\zeta_2=\zeta_3=\zeta_4=0.
\end{equation}
The domain of validity of this result encompasses isolated, weak, slowly moving, and weakly self-gravitating sources, which the isolated action $S_{\rm iso}$ satisfies completely. The exponential static ansatz provides an independent check of the relation $\beta=\gamma=1$, while its continuation to higher orders depends on the corresponding scalar source and action.

The duration of one complete clock cycle can also be expressed as the ratio of the ruler's coordinate footprint to the coordinate speed of light: $\Delta t\sim p_L/(c_0p_Tp_L)\propto p_T^{-1}$. Thus, a local light clock directly records the temporal metric component $p_T$. In the illustrative example of the common-scaling scheme where $p_T=p_L=1/2$, the clock period doubles while the coordinate speed of light decreases by a factor of four.

In a static, spherically symmetric field, the first post-Newtonian effects governed by $\beta=\gamma=1$ coincide with the classical predictions of General Relativity: gravitational light deflection, Shapiro radar time delay, gravitational redshift, and planetary perihelion precession \citep{bertotti2003,will2014}. In particular, the Cassini spacecraft radio-link experiment constrains the deviation $\gamma-1$ to $(2.1\pm2.3)\times10^{-5}$.

Historically, incorporating the spatial metric component $g_{ij}$ supplied the \textbf{factor of two} that replaced Einstein's 1911 half-deflection prediction with the full geometric result of 1915--1916 \citep{einstein1916,shapiro1964}. In the weak isotropic exterior region of $\RefG$, the first-order clock and ruler responses are equal, and their product naturally combines both contributions in the coordinate speed of light.

\subsection{Refractive Attraction, Inertia, and the Absence of Friction}

An oscillon, as a spatially extended wave structure, propagates through spatial gradients of foundation-medium pressure and coordinate wave speed. Its phase velocity is higher on the denser side and lower on the rarefied, pressure-deficit side. Because of this difference, by the Huygens--Fresnel principle and the eikonal equation, the wavefront bends toward the region of lower pressure and reduced coordinate speed---it undergoes \textbf{refraction}. In the weak, static limit, the pressure deficit created by a massive body establishes an effective optical-geometric medium, and the refracted trajectory of a matter wavepacket in this medium manifests macroscopically as gravitational attraction.

In $\RefG$, the free fall of a body in the Earth's gravitational field results from the refractive deflection of the oscillon structures constituting its atoms. This deflection is directed toward the region of lower pressure and slower coordinate propagation produced by the Earth. Gravitational attraction is thus the direct local response of wave-like matter to spatial inhomogeneity in the medium.

In the relational-coframe description, deformation of the foundation is recorded by the full tensor geometry. Its static temporal and spatial characteristics are $p_T$ and $p_L$, while the weak exterior common-$p$ approximation recovers the classical refractive picture. In the isolated action $S_{\rm iso}$, canonical matter and the Maxwell field each enter the Hilbert stress--energy tensor once through their own metric variation. The total asymptotic mass of a bound system combines the proper energy of matter and gravitational binding; for a system with positive binding energy $B$, the mass defect is $\Delta M=B/c_0^2$.

The gravitational-wave event GW150914 provides a direct observational example of this energy balance: the difference of approximately three solar masses ($3\,M_\odot$) between the asymptotic masses of the initial binary system and the final merged remnant was radiated as gravitational-wave energy \citep{abbott2016}. In the language of $\RefG$, two oscillatory sources reorganize into a single, deeper deficit profile, and in the teleparallel (TEGR) low-energy geometry this rapid nonlinear reorganization is described by the radiation of standard transverse-traceless tensor waves.

On the low-energy covariant branch of $\RefG$, the \textbf{inertia} of a body is determined by the total energy--momentum of the oscillon and its own gravitational field. Uniform rectilinear motion of the body is obtained by a Lorentz boost of the entire stationary solution, under which the matter phase, spatial profile, and surrounding geometry transform together. Under acceleration, an external force changes the total momentum of this unified system. The asymmetric rearrangement of the foundation's deficit profile provides the intuitive physical picture of this process, while the detailed mechanism of its nodal delay belongs to the microscopic dynamics of the foundation.

For an isolated, spatially localized solution, the relativistic dispersion and inertia relations hold exactly:
\begin{equation}
E(v)=\gamma E_0,\qquad
\mathbf P(v)=\gamma\frac{E_0}{c_0^2}\mathbf v,\qquad
M_I=\left.\frac{\partial P_\parallel}{\partial v}\right|_{v=0}
=\frac{E_0}{c_0^2}.
\end{equation}
In flat spacetime, this equality follows directly from the full matter stress tensor and Laue's equilibrium identity ($\int T^{ij}d^3x=0$), whereas for a self-gravitating body $E_0$ is the total asymptotic (ADM) energy of the system and the momentum is likewise determined by the asymptotic integral of the full metric. In uniform motion, the rest mass $E_0/c_0^2$ of the body is invariant while its total energy increases to $\gamma E_0$, and Lorentz length contraction along the direction of motion reflects the coordinate readout. In a weak, slowly varying external gravitational field, to monopole order, the active and passive gravitational masses also equal $E_0/c_0^2$ (rapid internal deformation, tidal forces, and radiative backreaction constitute higher-order corrections). The result of the MICROSCOPE space mission provides the most stringent observational test of the weak equivalence principle \citep{touboul2022}.

In uniform rectilinear motion, the oscillon's wave structure is transmitted symmetrically between foundation nodes without energy dissipation. $\RefG$ associates \textbf{frictionless inertial motion} with this regime of coherent phase transfer: displacement of the body through the nodal medium proceeds by successive transmission of its wave state. In the low-energy continuum limit, the common Lorentzian structure identifies the limiting speed of phase transmission directly with the metric light cone.

The equilibrium of a weakly bound oscillon computed in this same action is also confirmed by variational energy analysis: at fixed total phase charge, radial deformation of the profile in every tested direction increases the total energy of the system, and this property persists stably under grid refinement and outer-boundary expansion. This confirms the radial stability of the body, while the investigation of full three-dimensional nonlinear evolution remains a subsequent numerical task.

When the oscillon is analyzed within the unified covariant action together with the ambient collective field $\Psi_C$, three rigorous physical regimes of coupling to the medium emerge:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Infinite-Reservoir $1/r$ Divergence Theorem:} In asymptotically flat infinite space, a global frequency $\omega_C \ge \mu_{C0}$ cannot be maintained with finite energy: because of the oscillon's gravitational redshift ($N(r) \simeq 1 - GM/r < 1$), the local chemical potential $\omega_C/N(r)$ at large distances exceeds the threshold value $\mu_{C0}$, generating a $1/r$ asymptotic tail of the $\Psi_C$ field and causing the total charge and mass to diverge quadratically ($Q_C(R), M_C(R) \propto R^2 \to \infty$);
\item \textbf{Pure-Vacuum and Gravitating Trapped-Pocket Regimes:} When $\omega_C \le \mu_{C0} N_0(0)$, the ambient collective field is unexcited ($\Psi_C \equiv 0$) and all properties of the isolated oscillon remain unchanged; in the intermediate frequency window $\mu_{C0} N_0(0) < \omega_C < \mu_{C0}$, the collective field $\Psi_C$ condenses directly within the particle's gravitational potential well, forming a compact ``trapped pocket'' of finite radius ($r < r_{\rm trap}(\omega_C)$);
\item \textbf{Two-Charge First Law and Composite-Body Inertia:} In the trapped-pocket regime, the system obeys an exact two-charge variational first law:
\begin{equation}
dM_{\rm ADM} = \Omega\,dQ + \omega_C\,dQ_C.
\end{equation}
Because both fields ($\Psi_O$ and $\Psi_C$) are localized within a finite domain and the exterior spacetime is asymptotically flat, the inertial mass of the composite $(Q, Q_C)$ body again equals \textbf{exactly} its total gravitational energy: $M_I = M_{\rm ADM}(Q, Q_C)$. Calculating the hydrodynamic added mass and finite-domain boundary effects for a body moving through a nonvanishing, infinitely extended cosmological background ($\Psi_C(\infty) \neq 0$) constitutes a separate research problem.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Local Unobservability and the Principle of Relativity}

A local observer in a gravitational field measures time and spatial intervals in the orthonormal frame (basis) of the same metric to which matter and light are coupled. Consequently, the locally measured speed of light is always equal to $c_0$, even as the coordinate factors of clocks and rulers ($p_T$ and $p_L$) vary. Environmental changes that are slow relative to internal oscillation periods proceed adiabatically, while the response of a specific material spectrum and its dimensionless ratios is determined from the corresponding action and small-perturbation equations. The common-scaling picture describes this local self-calibration in the special case $p_T=p_L$.

Two classes of experiment establish the observational bounds on this mechanism:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Gravitational Redshift:} The Pound--Rebka experiment \citep{pound1960}, the Gravity Probe A hydrogen-maser experiment \citep{vessot1980}, and the Galileo satellite measurements \citep{delva2018,herrmann2018} confirm with high precision the dependence of clock rate on gravitational potential. $\RefG$ interprets this dependence as the temporal component of the common pressure--geometric response.
\item \textbf{Invariance of Dimensionless Ratios:} Absorption spectra of distant quasars and laboratory optical atomic clocks constrain possible cosmological variation of the fine-structure constant ($\alpha$) to the level $|\Delta\alpha/\alpha| \lesssim 10^{-6}$ \citep{kotus2017}, in full agreement with the principle of common-clock self-calibration.
\end{itemize}

The null result of the Michelson--Morley experiment reflects the medium's \textbf{common-mode self-calibration}, whereas signals registered by gravitational-wave detectors (LIGO, Virgo, KAGRA) measure a \textbf{differential tensor response}:
\begin{itemize}
\item Local clocks, rulers, and light signals are calibrated within the frame of one and the same metric. Modern optical resonators constrain local directional anisotropy in the speed of light to the level $\Delta c/c \lesssim 10^{-17}$ (\citealt{herrmann2009}).
\item A gravitational wave is a time-dependent, quadrupolar differential deformation ($h_+, h_\times$): it stretches space along one axis while compressing it along the perpendicular axis. This opposite-sign deformation breaks common-mode compensation and produces a measurable phase shift between the perpendicular arms of an interferometer.
\end{itemize}

FitzGerald--Lorentz length contraction \citep{fitzgerald1889,lorentz1892} is a historical antecedent of this reasoning. $\RefG$ relates the contraction to the fact that measuring instruments are themselves wave excitations of the foundation-medium.

In the TEGR-equivalent low-energy geometry, tensor waves propagate on the common metric light cone, so $v_{\rm gw}=c$ at this order. The nearly simultaneous detection of gravitational waves and gamma rays from the binary neutron-star merger GW170817 constrains their fractional speed difference \citep{abbott2017}:
\begin{equation}
-3 \times 10^{-15} \le \frac{v_{\rm gw} - c}{c} \le +7 \times 10^{-16}
\end{equation}
The orbital decay of relativistic binary pulsars (PSR B1913+16, PSR J0737-3039A/B) confirms the general-relativistic normalization of quadrupole energy loss to an accuracy of $10^{-3}$--$10^{-4}$ \citep{weisberg2016,kramer2021}. The LIGO--Virgo--KAGRA GWTC-3 catalogue also places stringent limits on the scalar breathing-polarization contribution \citep{ligovirgokagra2022}.

In the isolated-system and binary-pulsar regime ($\Psi_C \equiv 0$, $S_{\rm iso}$), linearizing the relational coframe about Minkowski space and integrating the Isaacson radiative stress-energy tensor over the celestial sphere yields three exact analytical and numerical results (verified across \texttt{18/18} checks):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Coframe Linearization, $v_{\rm gw} \equiv c_0$, and Two Transverse-Traceless Polarizations:} Expanding the tetrad into symmetric strain $h_{\mu\nu} = h_{\nu\mu}$ and antisymmetric local Lorentz rotation $\omega_{\mu\nu} = -\omega_{\nu\mu}$,
\begin{equation}
e^A{}_\mu = \delta^A_\mu + \frac{1}{2}\eta^{AB}h_{B\mu} + \frac{1}{2}\eta^{AB}\omega_{B\mu},
\end{equation}
the operational metric $g_{\mu\nu} = \eta_{AB}e^A{}_\mu e^B{}_\nu = \eta_{\mu\nu} + h_{\mu\nu} + \mathcal{O}(h^2,\omega^2)$ is identically independent of $\omega_{\mu\nu}$ at linear order ($\partial g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}/\partial\omega_{\alpha\beta} \equiv 0$). On $S_{\rm iso}$, the linearized Einstein tensor $G^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}[h]$ satisfies the contracted Bianchi identity $\partial^\mu G^{(1)}_{\mu\nu} \equiv 0$ identically and reduces in Lorenz gauge ($\partial^\mu\bar{h}_{\mu\nu} = 0$, $\bar{h}_{\mu\nu} = h_{\mu\nu} - \frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}h$) to
\begin{equation}
\Box\bar{h}_{\mu\nu} = \left(-\frac{1}{c_0^2}\frac{\partial^2}{\partial t^2} + \nabla^2\right)\bar{h}_{\mu\nu} = -\frac{16\pi G}{c_0^4}T_{\mu\nu}.
\end{equation}
In vacuum, the dispersion relation $\omega^2 = c_0^2|\mathbf{k}|^2$ gives $v_{\rm phase} = v_{\rm group} = c_0$ ($(v_{\rm gw}-c_0)/c_0 \equiv 0$, satisfying GW170817), while the four Lorenz gauge conditions and four residual harmonic gauge transformations ($\Box\xi_\mu = 0$) reduce the 10 components of $h_{\mu\nu}$ to $10 - 4 - 4 = 2$ orthogonal transverse-traceless (TT) polarizations ($h_+, h_\times$), with zero scalar breathing ($h_b = 0$), longitudinal ($h_L = 0$), and vector ($h_{vx} = h_{vy} = 0$) radiative modes. In an equal-arm Michelson interferometer, isotropic scalar clock--ruler perturbations cancel identically between perpendicular arms ($\Delta\tau_x - \Delta\tau_y \equiv 0$), whereas a TT wave produces the differential fractional strain $(\Delta L_x - \Delta L_y)/L_0 = F_+ h_+ + F_\times h_\times$.
\item \textbf{Isaacson Quadrupole Flux, Peters--Mathews Eccentric Enhancement, and Zero Dipole Radiation:} Averaging the TT projection tensor $\Lambda_{ij,kl}(\hat{\mathbf{n}})$ over the unit sphere yields $\langle\Lambda_{ij,kl}\rangle_{S^2} = \frac{2}{5}I^{\rm sym\text{-}tf}_{ijkl}$, from which the Isaacson radiative stress-energy tensor $t^{\rm GW}_{\mu\nu} = \frac{c_0^4}{32\pi G}\langle\partial_\mu h^{\rm TT}_{ij}\partial_\nu h^{\rm TT\,ij}\rangle$ integrates to the exact Einstein quadrupole luminosity and angular-momentum loss:
\begin{align}
P_{\rm GW} &= \frac{G}{5c_0^5}\left\langle\dddot{Q}_{ij}\dddot{Q}^{ij}\right\rangle = \frac{32 G^4 m_1^2 m_2^2(m_1+m_2)}{5 c_0^5 a^5}\,f(e), \nonumber\\
\left\langle\frac{dJ}{dt}\right\rangle &= -\frac{32 G^{7/2} m_1^2 m_2^2\sqrt{m_1+m_2}}{5 c_0^5 a^{7/2}}\,g(e),
\end{align}
with exact symbolic Peters--Mathews eccentric enhancement factors
\begin{equation}
f(e) = \frac{1 + \frac{73}{24}e^2 + \frac{37}{96}e^4}{(1-e^2)^{7/2}}, \qquad g(e) = \frac{1 + \frac{7}{8}e^2}{(1-e^2)^2}.
\end{equation}
Dividing $da/dt$ by $de/dt$ integrates in closed form to Peters' invariant $a(e) = c_0 e^{12/19}(1-e^2)\left[1 + \frac{121}{304}e^2\right]^{870/2299}$. Furthermore, because the two-charge inertia theorem ensures universal coupling ($M_I = M_{\rm ADM}$) with zero scalar sensitivity difference ($\Delta s_{12} = s_1 - s_2 \equiv 0$), dipolar gravitational radiation vanishes identically ($\dot{P}_b^{\rm dipole} \equiv 0$) for both neutron-star--neutron-star and neutron-star--white-dwarf binaries.
\item \textbf{Precision Binary Pulsar Benchmarks (PSR B1913+16, PSR J0737-3039A/B, PSR J1738+0333) and Standard Sirens:} Evaluating the orbital period decay rate $\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = -\frac{192\pi}{5}\left(\frac{2\pi G\mathcal{M}_c}{c_0^3 P_b}\right)^{5/3}f(e)$ against relativistic pulsar timing data yields:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Hulse--Taylor Pulsar (PSR B1913+16; $P_b = 0.322997448918\text{ d}$, $e = 0.6171340$, $m_1 = 1.438\,M_\odot$, $m_2 = 1.390\,M_\odot$):} $f(e) = 11.8568$, coalescence time $T_{\rm coal} = 300.70\text{ Myr}$, and $\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = -2.4021\times 10^{-12}\text{ s/s}$ vs.\ the galactic-acceleration-corrected observed value $\dot{P}_b^{\rm obs,corr} = (-2.398 \pm 0.004)\times 10^{-12}\text{ s/s}$, giving $\dot{P}_b^{\rm obs,corr}/\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = 0.9983 \pm 0.0017$ ($1.03\sigma$).
\item \textbf{Double Pulsar (PSR J0737-3039A/B; $P_b = 0.1022515592973\text{ d}$, $e = 0.0877770$, $m_A = 1.338185\,M_\odot$, $m_B = 1.248868\,M_\odot$, $R = m_A/m_B = 1.071518$):} $f(e) = 1.0515$ and $\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = -1.247810\times 10^{-12}\text{ s/s}$ vs.\ $\dot{P}_b^{\rm obs,corr} = (-1.247752 \pm 0.000079)\times 10^{-12}\text{ s/s}$ \citep{kramer2021}, giving $\dot{P}_b^{\rm obs,corr}/\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = 0.999953 \pm 0.000063$ (a relative difference of $4.68\times 10^{-5} < 10^{-4}$, $0.74\sigma$), while simultaneously matching the 1PN periastron advance ($\dot{\omega}^{\rm 1PN} = 16.8994^\circ/\text{yr}$), Einstein timing delay ($\gamma_E = 0.38400\text{ ms}$, $0.51\sigma$), and Shapiro range and shape ($r_{\rm Sh} = 6.151\,\mu\text{s}$, $0.51\sigma$; $s_{\rm Sh} = \sin i = 0.99992$).
\item \textbf{Asymmetric NS--WD Binary (PSR J1738+0333; $m_{\rm NS} = 1.46\,M_\odot$, $m_{\rm WD} = 0.181\,M_\odot$):} With $\dot{P}_b^{\rm dipole} \equiv 0$, the pure quadrupole prediction $\dot{P}_b^{\rm RefG} = -2.759\times 10^{-14}\text{ s/s}$ matches the observed intrinsic decay $(-2.59 \pm 0.32)\times 10^{-14}\text{ s/s}$ at $0.53\sigma$.
\item \textbf{Cosmological Tensor Propagation:} On the operational FLRW metric ($A = a/p = a^{5/2}$) with line element $ds^2 = A^2(\eta)[-c_0^2 d\eta^2 + (\delta_{ij} + h^{\rm TT}_{ij})dx^i dx^j]$, the Mukhanov--Sasaki variable $\chi_k = A\,h_k$ satisfies $\chi_k'' + (k^2 - A''/A)\chi_k = 0$ with zero Planck-mass running ($\alpha_M \equiv 0$), so the gravitational-wave luminosity distance equals the electromagnetic luminosity distance identically: $D_L^{\rm GW}(z) \equiv D_L^{\rm EM}(z)$.
\end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

With metric signature $(-+++)$, an operational invariant of this description is the difference between the proper times accumulated by two clocks along worldlines with different gravitational and kinematic histories:
\begin{equation}
\tau_a=\frac{1}{c}\int_{\gamma_a}\sqrt{-g_{\mu\nu}dx^\mu dx^\nu},
\qquad
\Delta\tau=\tau_1-\tau_2.
\end{equation}

The accumulated proper times are invariant records of the clocks' trajectories through different pressure regimes. Locally measured instantaneous rates and lengths are relative properties of the common medium.

\subsection{Historical Context and the Role of RefG}

Refractive descriptions of gravity in terms of an optical medium date to the development of general relativity. In 1911--1912, before its final geometric formulation, Albert Einstein related the coordinate speed of light to the gravitational potential \citep{einstein1911,einstein1912}:
\begin{equation}
c(\Phi) = c_0 \left(1 + \frac{\Phi}{c^2}\right)
\end{equation}
The optical properties of moving media had been studied earlier. In 1851, Armand Hippolyte Fizeau demonstrated partial light dragging in moving water, described by Fresnel's dragging coefficient (\citealt{fizeau1851}). In 1923, Walter Gordon showed that electromagnetic waves in a moving dielectric medium follow the null geodesics of an effective Lorentzian (pseudo-Riemannian) metric \citep{gordon1923}. This established the first rigorous mathematical connection between medium optics and spacetime geometry.

In 1957, Robert Dicke described gravitation through a variable vacuum refractive index \citep{dicke1957}. William Unruh's 1981 work established the basis of modern \textbf{analogue gravity}: acoustic waves in a moving fluid with a supersonic region propagate according to an effective metric with an acoustic black-hole horizon \citep{unruh1981}.

Grigory Volovik's condensed-matter program \citep{volovik2003} and the review by Barceló, Liberati, and Visser \citep{barcelo2011} showed how local Lorentz invariance and effective curved spacetime can emerge from the microscopic dynamics of background quantum condensates, including those in liquid helium-3 and helium-4. In 2019, Jeff Steinhauer's group experimentally observed the acoustic analogue of quantum thermal Hawking radiation in a rubidium Bose--Einstein condensate \citep{munozdenova2019}.

Related research explores the \textbf{thermodynamic and entropic origins} of gravity:
\begin{itemize}
\item Andrei Sakharov interpreted gravity as an elastic vacuum phenomenon induced by quantum fluctuations \citep{sakharov1967};
\item Ted Jacobson demonstrated that Einstein's field equations arise directly as an equation of state from the thermodynamic entropy balance ($\delta Q = T dS$) across local Rindler horizons \citep{jacobson1995};
\item Erik Verlinde described gravitational attraction as an entropic effect driven by changes in information on holographic screens \citep{verlinde2011}.
\end{itemize}

These approaches and $\RefG$ share the view that spacetime geometry is a derived physical structure. $\RefG$ takes a pressure--refractive foundation-medium as its physical source.

Refractive Gravity ($\RefG$) brings together four principles related to these approaches:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Monistic Ontological Unity:} The foundation-medium produces both the geometric response and the oscillon structures constituting matter, measuring instruments, and the observer. The hydrodynamic interpretation is thereby incorporated into a single calculable physical framework.
\item \textbf{Population-Locked Local Frequency Scale ($\Omega_t$):} Nonlinear synchronization provides a specific mechanism for local self-calibration. For a physical oscillon, it predicts a spectrum in which all stable-mode frequencies scale with the common pressure factor while their dimensionless ratios remain unchanged.
\item \textbf{One Foundation with a Multicomponent Geometric Response:} Pressure and expansion govern the scalar readout, symmetric deformation and shear determine the tensor response, and orientation remains an inertial gauge freedom. These components define one operational geometry. Each physical stress--energy contribution in the action couples to its metric once.
\item \textbf{From Relational Coframe to Einstein--Hilbert:} In the post-Genesis coframe's leading local, reversible, constant-coefficient, parity-even quadratic-torsion class, with minimal phase coupling and no unsuppressed mixed operator at that order, the regular linear Minkowski limit determines the TEGR coefficient ratios by requiring the absence of an independent physical orientation mode. The exact torsion--curvature identity converts the action to Einstein--Hilbert form up to a boundary term \citep{jimenez2020ngr,maluf2013tegr}. The Fierz--Pauli linear spin-2 construction and Deser's universal self-coupling provide a separate route to the same final operator. In four dimensions, Lovelock's theorem gives the corresponding uniqueness statement for local metric field equations of at most second differential order, up to normalization and a cosmological term \citep{deser1970,lovelock1971}.
\end{enumerate}

$\RefG$ combines optical refraction, topological solitons, and thermodynamic balance within one research program. Each connection is subject to its corresponding mathematical and observational tests.

\section{Three Scales and Cosmological Horizons}

\subsection{The Local Scale: Compact Objects, Internal State, and External Readout}

In this subsection, the description of strong gravitational fields unites two complementary levels: dynamical collapse in the canonical scalar--geometric sector, and the horizonless exponential exterior of the covariant medium, whose regular $C^2$ interior core closes directly from the action.

The pressure mechanism described in the preceding chapters is considered at three distinct scales:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Local scale:} the localized pressure-deficit profile surrounding oscillons and compact relativistic bodies;
\item \textbf{Galactic scale:} the coherent vortical flow of the foundation;
\item \textbf{Cosmological scale:} relaxation of the background pressure driven by geometric expansion of the foundation-medium, together with the relation between coordinate and process time.
\end{enumerate}

These are three dynamical regimes of one foundation. The local regime describes spatially inhomogeneous pressure-deficit profiles, the galactic regime describes the coherent long-range response, and the cosmological regime describes the global evolution of the foundation.

In an extreme gravitational regime, an object's \textbf{internal state} ($M_{\rm proper}$) and its \textbf{externally inferred active gravitational mass} ($M_{\rm external}$) are distinct quantities. The compact-object map of $\RefG$ distinguishes the conserved internal content of accumulated matter from the gravitational charge inferred at a distance. A remote observer measures the state through its pressure-deficit profile, clock rate, and changing operational size, while the interior geometry, horizon, and global causal structure are determined by the complete nonlinear field solution.

Within the equilibrium family computed by coupling the canonical oscillon field to Einstein geometry, the total mass of a bound system is smaller than the summed masses of isolated configurations carrying the same total phase charge. Equal increments of charge yield progressively smaller increments of external mass, while the central proper clock slows relative to a distant clock. The geometric determination of the external mass agrees with the full energy accounting, including binding. Total mass is thus a property of the jointly adjusted configuration.

$\RefG$ relates the mass defect of a merging compact system to nonlinear changes in binding and in the deficit profile. Its quantitative connection to the outgoing tensor flux is determined by the complete dynamical solution, with the gravitational-wave event GW150914 providing the experimental benchmark \citep{abbott2016}.

This self-consistent equilibrium has a concrete dynamical response. A weak inward perturbation that conserves phase charge first compresses the compact configuration, then arrests the contraction and produces expansion; the body continues to oscillate over the computed interval. Matter motion, the gravitational field, and the local clock rate evolve together under equations derived from a single action.

Stronger compression in the same model produces a trapped-light region: spherical light fronts sent in either radial direction decrease in area. The calculation continues beyond the formation of this region, tracking central proper time, density, and curvature together. The trapping geometry and the physical evolution of the interior are thus described by one dynamical solution (Figure~\ref{fig:strong-field-process}).

In the description of a regular interior state, the energy balance of the foundation and matter, the local clock rate, and the spatial scale are determined jointly. On the static common-scaling branch of the covariant medium, this structure closes analytically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Horizonless Exponential Exterior ($r \ge r_c$):} Outside the core ($r \ge r_c$), the exact static vacuum solution is the horizonless exponential metric
\begin{equation}
B(r) = e^{-2m/r}, \qquad A(r) = e^{2m/r}, \qquad H(r) = \frac{m}{r}, \qquad \ell(r) = r.
\end{equation}
This geometry has no event horizon ($B(r) > 0$ for all $r > 0$), and in the ultracompact regime ($q \equiv 2m/r_c > 1$) it possesses an outer unstable photon sphere at $r_{\rm ph}^+ = 2m$ with critical shadow impact parameter $b_c = 2e m \approx 5.4366 m$;
\item \textbf{Pure-Medium No-Go and Central-Matter Necessity Theorem:} The radial-label ($\ell$) equation takes the exact divergence form $\frac{d}{dr}(Q C_\ell) = \mathcal{S}_{\rm met}(r) - \frac{r^2\sqrt{AB}}{\ell^2}\frac{dF_{\rm med}}{d\widehat{I}_3}$. Across the horizonless ultracompact interval $1 < q < q_* \approx 1.9897$, the geometric source integral for a smooth degree-6 $C^2$ core is strictly positive ($\mathcal{I}_{\rm met}(q) > 0$; for instance, $\mathcal{I}_{\rm met}(3/2) \approx 0.14536$). This establishes a universal theorem: \textbf{a pure medium without matter ($T^{\rm m}_{\mu\nu} = 0$) cannot close a $C^2$ core across the boundary $r = r_c$ without a stress jump}---a singularity-free balanced $C^2$ core requires the energy density and pressure of compressed central matter itself ($T^{\rm m}_{\mu\nu} \neq 0$);
\item \textbf{Action-Derived $C^2$ Regular Core ($r \le r_c$):} When a covariantly conserved matter sector ($\nabla_\mu T^{\rm m\,\mu\nu} = 0$) is included in the core and the order-reduction condition at the boundary $r = r_c$ uniquely selects the relation $H(x) = -\frac{1}{2}\ell_B(x)$ ($\widehat{Y} \equiv 1$, with scalar charge profile $Q_H(0)=0$ and $Q_H(r_c)=m$), the boundary-value problem closes with zero defect ($Q C_\ell(r_c) = 0$; at the benchmark point $q = 3/2$, the central density root is $\rho_0^* \approx 0.66364$). At the center of the core ($r=0$), all curvature invariants and the clock rate are finite ($B(0) = e^{-q} > 0$, $A(0) = 1$, $K(0) < \infty$), the deformation invariants $\widehat{I}_1(x)$ and $\widehat{I}_3(x)$ are strictly monotonic (free of cavitation and gradient singularities), and a second, radially stable inner light ring appears within the core ($x_{\rm lr}(1.5) \approx 0.87058$);
\item \textbf{Quantitative EHT Shadow (Sgr A* and M87*), Eikonal Quasinormal Modes (QNM), and Gravitational-Wave Echo Bridge:} On the horizonless exponential exterior, the optical potential $V_+(r) = e^{-4m/r}/r^2$ attains its unique global maximum at $r_{\rm ph}^+ = 2m = r_s$ ($V_+''(2m) = -\frac{e^{-2}}{8 m^4} < 0$), yielding the critical shadow impact parameter $b_c^{\rm RefG} = 2 e m \approx 5.4366\,m$ and the parameter-free fractional shift relative to Schwarzschild ($b_c^{\rm GR} = 3\sqrt{3}\,m \approx 5.1962\,m$) of $\delta_{\rm sh}^{\rm RefG} \equiv \frac{2e}{3\sqrt{3}} - 1 \approx +4.63\%$. For \textbf{Sgr A*} ($\theta_g = 5.028\,\mu\text{as}$), $\RefG$ predicts $d_{\rm sh}^{\rm RefG} = 4e\,\theta_g = 54.67\,\mu\text{as}$, lying strictly within the EHT 2022 $1\sigma$ bounds (direct diameter $48.7 \pm 7.0\,\mu\text{as}$, $0.85\sigma < 1\sigma$; Keck/VLTI fractional shift $\delta_{\rm Sgr A^*} = -0.04^{+0.09}_{-0.10} \in [-0.14, +0.05]$, $0.96\sigma < 1\sigma$), while for \textbf{M87*} ($\theta_g = 3.8\,\mu\text{as}$) it predicts $d_{\rm sh}^{\rm RefG} = 41.32\,\mu\text{as}$, which is even closer to the EHT measurement ($42.0 \pm 3.0\,\mu\text{as}$, $0.23\sigma < 1\sigma$; $|\delta_{\rm M87^*}| \le 0.17$, $0.27\sigma < 1\sigma$) than the Schwarzschild value $39.49\,\mu\text{as}$ ($0.84\sigma$). Moreover, the radial optical refractive index $n_{\rm opt}(r) \equiv \frac{dr_*}{dr} = \sqrt{A(r)/B(r)}$ is globally $C^2$-smooth across $r = r_c$ ($n_{\rm opt}(0) = e^{q/2} < \infty$), and at the outer photon sphere $r_{\rm ph}^+ = 2m$ the circular null orbital angular velocity and principal Lyapunov exponent are identically equal---$\Omega_{\rm ph}^{\rm RefG} = \lambda_{\rm L}^{\rm RefG} = \frac{c_0}{2 e m}$ ($\frac{\lambda_{\rm L}^{\rm RefG}}{\Omega_{\rm ph}^{\rm RefG}} \equiv 1$)---so that the dimensionless eikonal quasinormal-mode quality factor is \textbf{100\% identical to General Relativity}:
\begin{equation}
Q_{\ell n}^{\rm RefG} \equiv \frac{\omega_R^{(\ell n)}}{2\,\omega_I^{(\ell n)}} = \frac{\ell + 1/2}{2(n + 1/2)} \equiv Q_{\ell n}^{\rm GR},
\end{equation}
while the tortoise optical depth from the regular center $r = 0$ to the photon sphere $r_{\rm ph}^+ = 2m$ is strictly finite:
\begin{equation}
\frac{L_{*,\rm ext}(q)}{m} = 2\left[e - \frac{e^q}{q} + \operatorname{Ei}(q) - \operatorname{Ei}(1)\right], \qquad \Delta t_{\rm echo}(q, M) = \frac{2 L_*(q)}{c_0} = \mathcal{I}_{\rm echo}(q)\,\frac{G M}{c_0^3}.
\end{equation}
At the canonical benchmark $q = 3/2$, one obtains $\mathcal{I}_{\rm echo}(3/2) = 15.5553$ (with inner-cavity factor $\mathcal{I}_{\rm cav}(3/2) = 6.2528$ to the stable inner light ring and effective compactness $\epsilon_{\rm eff} = e^{-\mathcal{I}_{\rm echo}/4} = 0.02047$), predicting concrete post-merger echo delay times of $\Delta t_{\rm echo} = 0.766\text{ ms}$ ($10\,M_\odot$), $2.299\text{ ms}$ ($30\,M_\odot$), $4.750\text{ ms}$ (GW150914 $62\,M_\odot$ remnant), and $318.27\text{ s}$ (Sgr A*). Beyond this static $C^2$ equilibrium and linear wave bridge, the dynamical arrest of time-dependent gravitational collapse at $r = r_c$, the Kerr-like rotational generalization, and the microscopic derivation of the core matter equation of state remain open research problems.
\end{enumerate}

Assessing singularity avoidance requires the local source, curvature measured in freely falling frames, and geodesic extendibility. Penrose's null-convergence condition $R_{\mu\nu}k^\mu k^\nu\ge0$ is related to the null energy condition (NEC) through Einstein's equation and must be considered together with the theorem's other global premises \citep{penrose1965}. A stable phase current satisfying $\rho_C+p_C>0$ remains an NEC-compatible source within its own action; the contribution of the additional medium source is determined by its separate variation.

Regular compact objects are an active subject of relativistic astrophysics. The Mazur--Mottola gravastar, for example, is a vacuum-condensate object without a singular point \citep{mazur2004}. Such alternatives may be distinguished from classical black holes by EHT shadow structure, quasinormal-mode spectra, and gravitational-wave echo limits \citep{eventhorizon2019,cardoso2019}. The compact-object picture of $\RefG$ is structurally related to this class and associates the internal state with the pressure--energy balance of the universal foundation.

\subsection{The Galactic Scale: Vortical Flow and MOND}

\paragraph{Observational Evidence.} In many disk galaxies, rotation curves at large radii do not follow the Keplerian decline expected from visible baryonic matter alone: their shapes vary, but they often flatten or decline more slowly than expected. Baryonic mass and asymptotic rotation speed obey a tight empirical relation---the baryonic Tully--Fisher relation ($M_b \propto v_f^4$; \citealt{tully1977,lelli2016}). An early result in this observational program was Vera Rubin and Kent Ford's measurement of Andromeda's rotation curve \citep{rubin1970}. In the \textbf{radial acceleration relation (RAR)}, 2,693 radial data points from 153 galaxies follow a single universal curve \citep{mcgaugh2016}.

The standard $\Lambda$CDM model accounts for these observations through approximately spheroidal cold-dark-matter halos. Modified Newtonian Dynamics (MOND), by contrast, introduces a universal acceleration threshold:
\begin{equation}
a_0 \approx 1.2 \times 10^{-10} \text{ m/s}^2,
\end{equation}
below which gravitational acceleration transitions from Newtonian $a_N$ to the deep-MOND regime $\sqrt{a_N a_0}$ \citep{milgrom1983}. Relativistic completions (such as TeVeS and Skordis--Złośnik models) incorporate additional dynamical vector and scalar fields \citep{bekenstein2004,skordis2021}.

\paragraph{The RefG Vortical Mechanism and Foundation-Medium Polarization.} At galactic scales, $\RefG$ relates the additional deficit to large-scale \textbf{vortical flow and induced polarization} of the foundation-medium. An ordered rotational regime deepens the central pressure deficit, while baryonic oscillons and the surrounding flow form one shared refractive profile.

At the macroscopic level, this response is described by a Ginzburg--Landau-type local effective potential:
\begin{equation}
W(g_v, g_N) = \frac{1}{3} g_v^3 + \frac{1}{2} g_N g_v^2 - a_0 g_N g_v,
\end{equation}
where $g_v$ is the foundation-medium response field, $g_N$ is the baryonic Newtonian acceleration, and $a_0 \approx 1.2 \times 10^{-10}\text{ m/s}^2$ is the universal transition acceleration scale. The variational equilibrium condition, $\partial W / \partial g_v = 0$, yields:
\begin{equation}
g \equiv g_N + g_v, \qquad g_N = \frac{g^2}{g + a_0}.
\end{equation}

In the deep asymptotic regime ($g \ll a_0$), this relation approaches $g^2 \simeq a_0 g_N$, yielding for circular orbits the Baryonic Tully--Fisher Relation ($v^4 = G M_b a_0$) without individual galaxy tuning parameters.

\paragraph{Observational Check on SPARC Data.} Distinct from the published 153-galaxy, 2,693-point RAR sample, the cuts $\Delta V/V<10\%$ and $V_{\rm obs}>20\text{ km/s}$ select 2,788 points from 163 SPARC galaxies. With fixed $a_0$ and fixed mass-to-light ratios ($\Upsilon_{\rm disk}=0.5$, $\Upsilon_{\rm bulge}=0.7$), and without galaxy-by-galaxy parameter fitting, this comparison gives an RMS scatter of $0.141\text{ dex}$ and a mean residual of $-0.012\text{ dex}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sparc_rar}).

With fixed $a_0$ and mass-to-light ratios, but no galaxy-specific fitting, the algebraic $\RefG$ curve obtained from $W(g_v,g_N)$ gives an RMS scatter of $0.141\text{ dex}$ and a mean residual of $-0.012\text{ dex}$.}
